  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I, uh... I suppose I'm here because of what's been happening at my apartment building. It's been going on for a while now, and honestly, it's just getting to be too much. I mean, I know for a fact—absolutely certain—that the people living around me, my neighbors, they're watching me. Every time I leave my apartment, every time I come back, they're keeping track. I hear their doors click, or I see the blinds twitch. And then, and this is the part that really gets me, they're slipping these notes about me to the management office. I see the papers. They leave them on the doors, or they slide them under. Now, everybody—my sister, the building manager, even the maintenance guy—they all keep telling me, \"Oh, it's just routine notices, it's just reminders about the trash or the water being shut off.\" But I know better. I can read between the lines. I know it's about me, tracking my comings and goings, reporting on me. It's just exhausting, you know? Always having to be on guard, always wondering who's writing what about you. I just couldn't take it anymore, so I figured I had to come talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear, related to these notes or your neighbors watching you?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I haven't heard any voices or anything like that. It's not like someone is whispering in my ear or shouting my name when no one is there. I mean, I hear the actual sounds they make, like I said, the doors clicking shut right as I walk past, or the floorboards creaking upstairs when they're pacing, waiting for me to leave. But it's not like... imaginary voices or sounds that aren't really there. I know some people get that, where they hear things, but for me, it's all very real, very physical things happening right in front of me. The notes are right there on the paper, the blinds are actually moving. I don't need to hear voices to know what they're doing because they're so obvious about it, you know? It's the actions, the constant surveillance, the little noises they make when they think I'm not paying attention. But no, no voices talking to me or anything like that. Just the reality of what they're up to, which is honestly loud enough on its own without needing any extra sounds in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there are any other plots or plans against you beyond your neighbors watching you and leaving notes?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think so. It's really just the people in the building. I mean, I've racked my brain trying to figure out if there's some bigger picture here, you know? Like, is it the city? Is it the government? But honestly, I don't see any evidence of that. It's just the neighbors. Like Mrs. Gable in 3B, she's always at her window. And that guy on the first floor, the one with the loud truck, he's definitely part of it. But I haven't noticed anyone following me when I go to the grocery store or anything like that. It's really localized to the building. Which almost makes it worse, if you think about it, because it's where I live. It's my home. I should feel safe there, but instead, I'm constantly analyzing every little thing they do. But outside of the building, no, I haven't seen any signs of a larger plot or anything. It's just those people, right there, right where I have to sleep and eat and try to live my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop your neighbors from watching you and leaving these notes, or do you think this is something that will continue no matter what?\n\nPatient: Well, see, that's the thing, Doctor, and it's exactly why I'm here. I don't know how to stop it. I've thought about it, believe me, I have spent hours, days even, just sitting in my living room, staring at the wall, trying to figure out a way to make it stop. I've thought about confronting them, maybe knocking on Mrs. Gable's door and just flat out asking her, \"Why are you doing this?\" But then I think, well, what if that just makes it worse? What if they escalate it? Because, like I said when I first came in, I *know* they're watching my comings and goings. I know it in my bones. Every time I step out into the hallway, they are keeping tabs. And those notes... I know they're slipping notes about me to the office. It's not just a feeling, it's a fact. I see the papers. And it's so frustrating because, like I told you, everybody—my sister, the manager, the maintenance guy—they all keep telling me it's just notices on the doors. They say, \"Oh, it's just about the water,\" or \"It's just the trash schedule.\" But they don't see what I see. They don't live there. They don't feel the eyes on them every time they take the trash out. So, to answer your question, I really don't know if there's a way to stop it. If the management office is in on it, or at least accepting these notes and acting like they're just routine flyers... how do you fight that? It feels like it's just going to keep happening, and I'm just going to have to keep living like a prisoner in my own apartment, jumping at every creak of the floorboards and every click of a lock. It's just... it's a lot, you know? It's just a whole lot to carry around every single day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your neighbors' behavior towards you will ever change?\n\nPatient: Change? No... no, I don't see how it could change. Why would they change? They've got this whole system figured out, you know? Like I was saying before, that's exactly why I came in here to talk to someone, because I know—I *know*—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. They've made it their full-time job. And slipping those notes about me to the office... they aren't going to just wake up one morning and decide to stop doing that. They've got a routine down. They watch, they write, they slip the note. \n\nAnd the hardest part, the part that really makes me think it's never going to stop, is that nobody else seems to care or believe me. Even though everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors... my sister, the manager, everybody. They just brush it off. \"Oh, it's just a reminder about the parking lot,\" or \"Oh, they're just painting the lines next week.\" But I know what those papers really are. I know what they're doing. And if the office is just playing along, pretending they're regular notices, why would the neighbors ever stop? They have no reason to. They're getting away with it right out in the open. \n\nI just... I sit there and I think about how long this can go on. Years? Am I going to be dealing with Mrs. Gable staring at me when I'm 70? 80? Because they don't get tired. It's like they feed off of it. Every time I open my door, I can feel the shift in the hallway, like the air gets heavy because they're all suddenly on alert. And then another note goes to the office. So no, Doctor, I don't think they'll change. I think this is just how it is now, and I'm the only one who sees it for what it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your neighbors might not actually be watching you or leaving notes about you?\n\nPatient: No, I... I can't even entertain that idea, Doctor. I just can't. Because, like I've been trying to explain, and maybe I'm just not saying it right, but I came in here specifically because I know—I absolutely know—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It's not a possibility that they aren't. It's a reality. I live it every single day. I wake up, I make my coffee, and I know that as soon as I open my door to get the paper, someone is listening. Someone is watching. \n\nAnd those notes... I mean, how can I ignore what's right in front of my face? I see them slipping notes about me to the office. I've seen them walking down to the manager's door with papers in their hands. And it's just so exhausting, because I'm the only one who gets it. Like I said, everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister, she came over last Tuesday, right? And there was a paper taped to the door across the hall, and she just glanced at it and said, \"Oh, they're checking the smoke detectors next week.\" But I tried to tell her, I said, \"No, look at the way it's folded. Look at the time they put it up.\" It's a signal. It's information about me. But she just sighed and told me I was overthinking it. \n\nBut I'm not overthinking it. I'm just paying attention. I have to pay attention, because no one else is looking out for me. If I start second-guessing myself, if I start thinking, \"Oh, maybe it really is just a notice about the water being shut off,\" then I drop my guard. And I can't drop my guard. I just can't. The evidence is there, Doctor. It's in the way Mrs. Gable pulls her curtains back just an inch when I walk down the sidewalk. It's in the way the guy with the loud truck revs his engine right when I turn off my lights at night. They are watching me, and they are reporting on me to the office. I know it. I don't just think it, I know it. So no, there's no possibility I'm wrong about this. None at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over your daily life despite what's going on with your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Control? No... no, Doctor, I don't have any control left. It's slipping right through my fingers, to be honest with you. I try, I really do try to keep things normal, but I just... I can't keep my days organized anymore. It's like my brain is just a big tangled knot. Because of all this, you know, the watching and the notes, I've fallen way behind on my bills. There's a whole stack of mail just sitting on my kitchen table, right next to the salt shaker, and I know the electric company has sent at least two past-due notices, probably the water company too, but I just can't bring myself to open them. I just sit there looking at the envelopes. \n\nAnd it's not just the bills. I've missed appointments, too. I was supposed to get my teeth looked at three weeks ago, and I had a check-up with the foot doctor, but I just didn't go. I couldn't leave the apartment. I couldn't bring myself to walk past their doors. \n\nAnd the worst part, the part that really gets to me, is that I've completely stopped showing up for church. I used to go every single Sunday, sat in the same pew near the back. I even had volunteer commitments, you know? I used to help out sorting the canned goods for the community pantry on Thursday mornings. I liked doing that, making sure the green beans and the soup were all lined up nice, handing out boxes to folks who needed them. But I had to stop all of it. I just stopped showing up. Because... well, how can I go? I can't trust ordinary interactions anymore. I go down to the pantry, and someone says 'Good morning, how are you doing?' and suddenly I'm thinking, 'Why are you asking me that? Have you been talking to Mrs. Gable? Do you know what the office is putting in my file?' I just can't trust anybody to just be a regular person anymore. It's all tainted. Every conversation feels like a trap. So I stay home. I stay home, and I miss my appointments, and the bills pile up, and the church folks probably wonder where I went, because I just can't keep my life together while all this is happening. I just don't have any control over it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts or beliefs that you think might be true, but others around you don't seem to agree with?\n\nPatient: Other thoughts? Like... like what do you mean? No, I don't think so. I mean, isn't this enough? The whole thing with the neighbors and the office, that takes up every single ounce of my energy. I don't have the time or the brainpower to be sitting around focusing on other things. I'm just a regular guy, you know? Or, well, I was a regular guy before they made my life so complicated. \n\nIf you're asking if I have any, I don't know, crazy ideas or something... no, absolutely not. I know some folks get it in their heads that they're somebody famous, or that the FBI is tapping their phones, or that the TV is sending them secret messages. I had a cousin once who thought the radio was telling him how to play the lottery, bless his heart, but I'm not like that. I don't have some wild imagination. I just see what's right in front of my face. I'm a very practical person, really. Always have been. \n\nIf Mrs. Gable would just close her curtains, and if the office would just stop taking those notes about me, I wouldn't be sitting here talking to you about any of this. I'd be back at the church pantry, sorting the canned goods, minding my own business. I'd be paying my water bill and getting my teeth checked. I don't have any other strange thoughts. There's just the truth about what's happening in my building, and that's exactly what I've been trying to get people to understand. It's just this one thing, but it's... it's everything, you know? It's my whole life right now. I can't even think about anything else because from the minute I wake up until the minute I try to sleep, I'm just trying to survive the watching. So no, Doctor, nothing else. Just them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your neighbors watching you?\n\nPatient: Evidence? What kind of evidence could there even be, Doctor? I mean, like I was trying to explain earlier, I came in here because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. I know it for a fact. I see them slipping notes about me to the office. And I know everybody keeps telling me it is just notices on the doors, you know, regular building maintenance or whatever, but they just do not understand what is really going on. How could any evidence change my mind when I am living it every single day? \n\nIf it was just regular notices, why do they only seem to put them up when I am walking down the hall? Why is it always right after I get back from the grocery store? I will come back with my bags, just trying to get my milk and my eggs in the fridge before they go bad, and there is someone hovering near the office door with a piece of paper. You just cannot show me a piece of evidence that erases what my own two eyes are seeing. People try, believe me, they try all the time. My sister, the property manager, they all try to tell me I am getting worked up over nothing, over regular flyers. But they do not live in my shoes. They do not hear the floorboards creaking above me right when I turn on the television, or see the exact way those papers are folded to hide what is written inside. \n\nSo no, I just... I do not see how anything could change my mind, because the evidence is already there proving what I am saying. I came in here hoping someone would finally understand that I am being watched, and that the notes are about me, not to be talked out of it by someone who is not even there to see it happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you imagine any situation where you might consider the possibility that your neighbors are not watching you?\n\nPatient: Imagine a situation? Doctor, I... I feel like we're just going around in circles here. I really do. How can I imagine a situation where they aren't watching me when I am living in the middle of it every single day? \n\nLike I just got done saying, I came in here today, to this very clinic, specifically because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It's not a 'possibility' I can just turn off in my head like a light switch, or some game of pretend. I walk out my door, say, to take the trash down to the chute at the end of the hall. It's a Tuesday, maybe two in the afternoon, which should be a quiet time, right? Nobody should be around. But the exact second my deadbolt clicks, there's a door cracking open down the hall. And then I see them slipping those notes about me to the office. \n\nAnd I know... believe me, I know that everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister says it, the property manager says it, and now you're asking me to imagine they aren't doing it. 'Oh, it's just a reminder about the water being shut off for maintenance,' they say. 'Oh, it's just a flyer for the community watch meeting.' A flyer? Really? You think they need to fold a community flyer three times and slide it directly into the manager's hand the exact second I walk past the stairwell? I might not have finished twelfth grade, Doctor, but I am not a stupid man. I know what a regular piece of mail looks like, and I know what people look like when they're keeping tabs on someone. \n\nSo, no. No, I absolutely cannot imagine a situation where I'd think otherwise. Because to imagine that, I'd have to pretend my own two eyes don't work. I'd have to pretend I don't see the way they stop talking when I get off the elevator. I see the watching, I see the notes going straight to the office, and I just... I can't sit here and play make-believe that it's all just innocent door notices when they are documenting my whole life. I just can't do it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A fact? Well, yes, it is a fact. It is the absolute truth. I know you are trying to use these doctor words with me, asking if I hold onto it strongly or whatever, but... but look, like I've been telling you since I first sat down in this chair, the whole reason I came in here today is because I know—I absolutely *know*—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It is not just some belief I cooked up, it is my actual life. 

You talk about contradictory evidence, but what evidence are you talking about? The things everyone else tries to tell me? Because, yes, I know everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. I hear it all the time. They say, 'Oh, it's just the exterminator schedule,' or 'Oh, it's just a reminder about the recycling bins.' But they aren't the ones standing in the hallway when Mrs. Gable cracks her door open two inches just as I'm walking to the elevator. They aren't the ones feeling those eyes on the back of their neck.

I see them slipping notes about me to the office. I see the way the paper is folded up tight, and I see the looks they give the manager when they hand them over. So, yes, I hold onto it strongly, because it's what is actually happening right in front of my face. You wouldn't let someone tell you that the sky is green when you are looking right at it and seeing that it's blue, would you? That is exactly what this is like for me. Every single time I leave my apartment, every time I come back from checking my mail or just going out for a walk to clear my head, there they are. Watching. Documenting. And then passing those notes along to the office. 

So no matter how many times people try to contradict me and say it's just innocent door flyers, I know the truth. I'm living the truth. I just wish someone would finally listen to me instead of trying to convince me I'm not seeing what I'm seeing.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I, uh... I suppose I'm here because of what's been happening at my apartment building. It's been going on for a while now, and honestly, it's just getting to be too much. I mean, I know for a fact—absolutely certain—that the people living around me, my neighbors, they're watching me. Every time I leave my apartment, every time I come back, they're keeping track. I hear their doors click, or I see the blinds twitch. And then, and this is the part that really gets me, they're slipping these notes about me to the management office. I see the papers. They leave them on the doors, or they slide them under. Now, everybody—my sister, the building manager, even the maintenance guy—they all keep telling me, \"Oh, it's just routine notices, it's just reminders about the trash or the water being shut off.\" But I know better. I can read between the lines. I know it's about me, tracking my comings and goings, reporting on me. It's just exhausting, you know? Always having to be on guard, always wondering who's writing what about you. I just couldn't take it anymore, so I figured I had to come talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear, related to these notes or your neighbors watching you?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I haven't heard any voices or anything like that. It's not like someone is whispering in my ear or shouting my name when no one is there. I mean, I hear the actual sounds they make, like I said, the doors clicking shut right as I walk past, or the floorboards creaking upstairs when they're pacing, waiting for me to leave. But it's not like... imaginary voices or sounds that aren't really there. I know some people get that, where they hear things, but for me, it's all very real, very physical things happening right in front of me. The notes are right there on the paper, the blinds are actually moving. I don't need to hear voices to know what they're doing because they're so obvious about it, you know? It's the actions, the constant surveillance, the little noises they make when they think I'm not paying attention. But no, no voices talking to me or anything like that. Just the reality of what they're up to, which is honestly loud enough on its own without needing any extra sounds in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there are any other plots or plans against you beyond your neighbors watching you and leaving notes?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think so. It's really just the people in the building. I mean, I've racked my brain trying to figure out if there's some bigger picture here, you know? Like, is it the city? Is it the government? But honestly, I don't see any evidence of that. It's just the neighbors. Like Mrs. Gable in 3B, she's always at her window. And that guy on the first floor, the one with the loud truck, he's definitely part of it. But I haven't noticed anyone following me when I go to the grocery store or anything like that. It's really localized to the building. Which almost makes it worse, if you think about it, because it's where I live. It's my home. I should feel safe there, but instead, I'm constantly analyzing every little thing they do. But outside of the building, no, I haven't seen any signs of a larger plot or anything. It's just those people, right there, right where I have to sleep and eat and try to live my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop your neighbors from watching you and leaving these notes, or do you think this is something that will continue no matter what?\n\nPatient: Well, see, that's the thing, Doctor, and it's exactly why I'm here. I don't know how to stop it. I've thought about it, believe me, I have spent hours, days even, just sitting in my living room, staring at the wall, trying to figure out a way to make it stop. I've thought about confronting them, maybe knocking on Mrs. Gable's door and just flat out asking her, \"Why are you doing this?\" But then I think, well, what if that just makes it worse? What if they escalate it? Because, like I said when I first came in, I *know* they're watching my comings and goings. I know it in my bones. Every time I step out into the hallway, they are keeping tabs. And those notes... I know they're slipping notes about me to the office. It's not just a feeling, it's a fact. I see the papers. And it's so frustrating because, like I told you, everybody—my sister, the manager, the maintenance guy—they all keep telling me it's just notices on the doors. They say, \"Oh, it's just about the water,\" or \"It's just the trash schedule.\" But they don't see what I see. They don't live there. They don't feel the eyes on them every time they take the trash out. So, to answer your question, I really don't know if there's a way to stop it. If the management office is in on it, or at least accepting these notes and acting like they're just routine flyers... how do you fight that? It feels like it's just going to keep happening, and I'm just going to have to keep living like a prisoner in my own apartment, jumping at every creak of the floorboards and every click of a lock. It's just... it's a lot, you know? It's just a whole lot to carry around every single day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your neighbors' behavior towards you will ever change?\n\nPatient: Change? No... no, I don't see how it could change. Why would they change? They've got this whole system figured out, you know? Like I was saying before, that's exactly why I came in here to talk to someone, because I know—I *know*—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. They've made it their full-time job. And slipping those notes about me to the office... they aren't going to just wake up one morning and decide to stop doing that. They've got a routine down. They watch, they write, they slip the note. \n\nAnd the hardest part, the part that really makes me think it's never going to stop, is that nobody else seems to care or believe me. Even though everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors... my sister, the manager, everybody. They just brush it off. \"Oh, it's just a reminder about the parking lot,\" or \"Oh, they're just painting the lines next week.\" But I know what those papers really are. I know what they're doing. And if the office is just playing along, pretending they're regular notices, why would the neighbors ever stop? They have no reason to. They're getting away with it right out in the open. \n\nI just... I sit there and I think about how long this can go on. Years? Am I going to be dealing with Mrs. Gable staring at me when I'm 70? 80? Because they don't get tired. It's like they feed off of it. Every time I open my door, I can feel the shift in the hallway, like the air gets heavy because they're all suddenly on alert. And then another note goes to the office. So no, Doctor, I don't think they'll change. I think this is just how it is now, and I'm the only one who sees it for what it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your neighbors might not actually be watching you or leaving notes about you?\n\nPatient: No, I... I can't even entertain that idea, Doctor. I just can't. Because, like I've been trying to explain, and maybe I'm just not saying it right, but I came in here specifically because I know—I absolutely know—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It's not a possibility that they aren't. It's a reality. I live it every single day. I wake up, I make my coffee, and I know that as soon as I open my door to get the paper, someone is listening. Someone is watching. \n\nAnd those notes... I mean, how can I ignore what's right in front of my face? I see them slipping notes about me to the office. I've seen them walking down to the manager's door with papers in their hands. And it's just so exhausting, because I'm the only one who gets it. Like I said, everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister, she came over last Tuesday, right? And there was a paper taped to the door across the hall, and she just glanced at it and said, \"Oh, they're checking the smoke detectors next week.\" But I tried to tell her, I said, \"No, look at the way it's folded. Look at the time they put it up.\" It's a signal. It's information about me. But she just sighed and told me I was overthinking it. \n\nBut I'm not overthinking it. I'm just paying attention. I have to pay attention, because no one else is looking out for me. If I start second-guessing myself, if I start thinking, \"Oh, maybe it really is just a notice about the water being shut off,\" then I drop my guard. And I can't drop my guard. I just can't. The evidence is there, Doctor. It's in the way Mrs. Gable pulls her curtains back just an inch when I walk down the sidewalk. It's in the way the guy with the loud truck revs his engine right when I turn off my lights at night. They are watching me, and they are reporting on me to the office. I know it. I don't just think it, I know it. So no, there's no possibility I'm wrong about this. None at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over your daily life despite what's going on with your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Control? No... no, Doctor, I don't have any control left. It's slipping right through my fingers, to be honest with you. I try, I really do try to keep things normal, but I just... I can't keep my days organized anymore. It's like my brain is just a big tangled knot. Because of all this, you know, the watching and the notes, I've fallen way behind on my bills. There's a whole stack of mail just sitting on my kitchen table, right next to the salt shaker, and I know the electric company has sent at least two past-due notices, probably the water company too, but I just can't bring myself to open them. I just sit there looking at the envelopes. \n\nAnd it's not just the bills. I've missed appointments, too. I was supposed to get my teeth looked at three weeks ago, and I had a check-up with the foot doctor, but I just didn't go. I couldn't leave the apartment. I couldn't bring myself to walk past their doors. \n\nAnd the worst part, the part that really gets to me, is that I've completely stopped showing up for church. I used to go every single Sunday, sat in the same pew near the back. I even had volunteer commitments, you know? I used to help out sorting the canned goods for the community pantry on Thursday mornings. I liked doing that, making sure the green beans and the soup were all lined up nice, handing out boxes to folks who needed them. But I had to stop all of it. I just stopped showing up. Because... well, how can I go? I can't trust ordinary interactions anymore. I go down to the pantry, and someone says 'Good morning, how are you doing?' and suddenly I'm thinking, 'Why are you asking me that? Have you been talking to Mrs. Gable? Do you know what the office is putting in my file?' I just can't trust anybody to just be a regular person anymore. It's all tainted. Every conversation feels like a trap. So I stay home. I stay home, and I miss my appointments, and the bills pile up, and the church folks probably wonder where I went, because I just can't keep my life together while all this is happening. I just don't have any control over it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts or beliefs that you think might be true, but others around you don't seem to agree with?\n\nPatient: Other thoughts? Like... like what do you mean? No, I don't think so. I mean, isn't this enough? The whole thing with the neighbors and the office, that takes up every single ounce of my energy. I don't have the time or the brainpower to be sitting around focusing on other things. I'm just a regular guy, you know? Or, well, I was a regular guy before they made my life so complicated. \n\nIf you're asking if I have any, I don't know, crazy ideas or something... no, absolutely not. I know some folks get it in their heads that they're somebody famous, or that the FBI is tapping their phones, or that the TV is sending them secret messages. I had a cousin once who thought the radio was telling him how to play the lottery, bless his heart, but I'm not like that. I don't have some wild imagination. I just see what's right in front of my face. I'm a very practical person, really. Always have been. \n\nIf Mrs. Gable would just close her curtains, and if the office would just stop taking those notes about me, I wouldn't be sitting here talking to you about any of this. I'd be back at the church pantry, sorting the canned goods, minding my own business. I'd be paying my water bill and getting my teeth checked. I don't have any other strange thoughts. There's just the truth about what's happening in my building, and that's exactly what I've been trying to get people to understand. It's just this one thing, but it's... it's everything, you know? It's my whole life right now. I can't even think about anything else because from the minute I wake up until the minute I try to sleep, I'm just trying to survive the watching. So no, Doctor, nothing else. Just them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your neighbors watching you?\n\nPatient: Evidence? What kind of evidence could there even be, Doctor? I mean, like I was trying to explain earlier, I came in here because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. I know it for a fact. I see them slipping notes about me to the office. And I know everybody keeps telling me it is just notices on the doors, you know, regular building maintenance or whatever, but they just do not understand what is really going on. How could any evidence change my mind when I am living it every single day? \n\nIf it was just regular notices, why do they only seem to put them up when I am walking down the hall? Why is it always right after I get back from the grocery store? I will come back with my bags, just trying to get my milk and my eggs in the fridge before they go bad, and there is someone hovering near the office door with a piece of paper. You just cannot show me a piece of evidence that erases what my own two eyes are seeing. People try, believe me, they try all the time. My sister, the property manager, they all try to tell me I am getting worked up over nothing, over regular flyers. But they do not live in my shoes. They do not hear the floorboards creaking above me right when I turn on the television, or see the exact way those papers are folded to hide what is written inside. \n\nSo no, I just... I do not see how anything could change my mind, because the evidence is already there proving what I am saying. I came in here hoping someone would finally understand that I am being watched, and that the notes are about me, not to be talked out of it by someone who is not even there to see it happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you imagine any situation where you might consider the possibility that your neighbors are not watching you?\n\nPatient: Imagine a situation? Doctor, I... I feel like we're just going around in circles here. I really do. How can I imagine a situation where they aren't watching me when I am living in the middle of it every single day? \n\nLike I just got done saying, I came in here today, to this very clinic, specifically because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It's not a 'possibility' I can just turn off in my head like a light switch, or some game of pretend. I walk out my door, say, to take the trash down to the chute at the end of the hall. It's a Tuesday, maybe two in the afternoon, which should be a quiet time, right? Nobody should be around. But the exact second my deadbolt clicks, there's a door cracking open down the hall. And then I see them slipping those notes about me to the office. \n\nAnd I know... believe me, I know that everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister says it, the property manager says it, and now you're asking me to imagine they aren't doing it. 'Oh, it's just a reminder about the water being shut off for maintenance,' they say. 'Oh, it's just a flyer for the community watch meeting.' A flyer? Really? You think they need to fold a community flyer three times and slide it directly into the manager's hand the exact second I walk past the stairwell? I might not have finished twelfth grade, Doctor, but I am not a stupid man. I know what a regular piece of mail looks like, and I know what people look like when they're keeping tabs on someone. \n\nSo, no. No, I absolutely cannot imagine a situation where I'd think otherwise. Because to imagine that, I'd have to pretend my own two eyes don't work. I'd have to pretend I don't see the way they stop talking when I get off the elevator. I see the watching, I see the notes going straight to the office, and I just... I can't sit here and play make-believe that it's all just innocent door notices when they are documenting my whole life. I just can't do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your neighbors watching you could be considered a fact that you hold onto strongly, even if presented with contradictory evidence?\n\nPatient: A fact? Well, yes, it is a fact. It is the absolute truth. I know you are trying to use these doctor words with me, asking if I hold onto it strongly or whatever, but... but look, like I've been telling you since I first sat down in this chair, the whole reason I came in here today is because I know—I absolutely *know*—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It is not just some belief I cooked up, it is my actual life. \n\nYou talk about contradictory evidence, but what evidence are you talking about? The things everyone else tries to tell me? Because, yes, I know everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. I hear it all the time. They say, 'Oh, it's just the exterminator schedule,' or 'Oh, it's just a reminder about the recycling bins.' But they aren't the ones standing in the hallway when Mrs. Gable cracks her door open two inches just as I'm walking to the elevator. They aren't the ones feeling those eyes on the back of their neck.\n\nI see them slipping notes about me to the office. I see the way the paper is folded up tight, and I see the looks they give the manager when they hand them over. So, yes, I hold onto it strongly, because it's what is actually happening right in front of my face. You wouldn't let someone tell you that the sky is green when you are looking right at it and seeing that it's blue, would you? That is exactly what this is like for me. Every single time I leave my apartment, every time I come back from checking my mail or just going out for a walk to clear my head, there they are. Watching. Documenting. And then passing those notes along to the office. \n\nSo no matter how many times people try to contradict me and say it's just innocent door flyers, I know the truth. I'm living the truth. I just wish someone would finally listen to me instead of trying to convince me I'm not seeing what I'm seeing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your neighbors watching I know it for a fact. I see them slipping notes about me to the office. You just cannot show me a piece of evidence that erases what my own two eyes are seeing. People try, believe me, they try all the time. A larger plan? Well, what do you call it when a whole building is keeping track of one man? I don't know if it goes all the way to the mayor or the police or anything crazy like that, if that's what you mean by a larger plan. I'm just a regular guy, fifty-nine years old, I keep to myself, I don't bother anybody. But it takes a plan to do what they're doing, doesn't it? 

Like I have been saying this entire time, Doctor, the whole reason I came in here is because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. You can't just do that by accident. It takes coordination. They have to know when I'm going down to the laundry room in the basement, or when I'm walking to the corner store to get a pack of cigarettes, or when I'm taking out the trash. They have to be paying attention. 

And then, right on cue, there they are, slipping notes about me to the office. I see them do it with my own eyes. Just yesterday, I went down to check my mail—box 4B—and it took me maybe three minutes, tops. But sure enough, the guy from 4A is suddenly out there in the lobby, pretending to fiddle with his keys, and then he slides this little folded-up paper right to the manager the second my back is turned. 

And you ask if most people wouldn't believe it? Well, obviously they don't! That is the most frustrating part of this whole nightmare. Everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister, the neighbors who aren't in on it, even you keep asking me if I'm sure. They say, 'Oh, it's just the fire inspection schedule,' or 'It's just the landscaping notice for the parking lot.' But how does a landscaping notice explain them watching my door every time the deadbolt clicks? How does it explain the whispering when I get on the elevator? 

So yes, maybe people don't believe it, maybe they think it's unusual or crazy, but that doesn't change the fact that I am the one living it. I'm the one trapped in the middle of it. I just don't understand why it's so hard for anyone to just listen to what I'm telling them instead of treating me like I don't know the difference between a regular building flyer and a whole group of people keeping tabs on my life.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I, uh... I suppose I'm here because of what's been happening at my apartment building. It's been going on for a while now, and honestly, it's just getting to be too much. I mean, I know for a fact—absolutely certain—that the people living around me, my neighbors, they're watching me. Every time I leave my apartment, every time I come back, they're keeping track. I hear their doors click, or I see the blinds twitch. And then, and this is the part that really gets me, they're slipping these notes about me to the management office. I see the papers. They leave them on the doors, or they slide them under. Now, everybody—my sister, the building manager, even the maintenance guy—they all keep telling me, \"Oh, it's just routine notices, it's just reminders about the trash or the water being shut off.\" But I know better. I can read between the lines. I know it's about me, tracking my comings and goings, reporting on me. It's just exhausting, you know? Always having to be on guard, always wondering who's writing what about you. I just couldn't take it anymore, so I figured I had to come talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear, related to these notes or your neighbors watching you?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I haven't heard any voices or anything like that. It's not like someone is whispering in my ear or shouting my name when no one is there. I mean, I hear the actual sounds they make, like I said, the doors clicking shut right as I walk past, or the floorboards creaking upstairs when they're pacing, waiting for me to leave. But it's not like... imaginary voices or sounds that aren't really there. I know some people get that, where they hear things, but for me, it's all very real, very physical things happening right in front of me. The notes are right there on the paper, the blinds are actually moving. I don't need to hear voices to know what they're doing because they're so obvious about it, you know? It's the actions, the constant surveillance, the little noises they make when they think I'm not paying attention. But no, no voices talking to me or anything like that. Just the reality of what they're up to, which is honestly loud enough on its own without needing any extra sounds in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there are any other plots or plans against you beyond your neighbors watching you and leaving notes?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think so. It's really just the people in the building. I mean, I've racked my brain trying to figure out if there's some bigger picture here, you know? Like, is it the city? Is it the government? But honestly, I don't see any evidence of that. It's just the neighbors. Like Mrs. Gable in 3B, she's always at her window. And that guy on the first floor, the one with the loud truck, he's definitely part of it. But I haven't noticed anyone following me when I go to the grocery store or anything like that. It's really localized to the building. Which almost makes it worse, if you think about it, because it's where I live. It's my home. I should feel safe there, but instead, I'm constantly analyzing every little thing they do. But outside of the building, no, I haven't seen any signs of a larger plot or anything. It's just those people, right there, right where I have to sleep and eat and try to live my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop your neighbors from watching you and leaving these notes, or do you think this is something that will continue no matter what?\n\nPatient: Well, see, that's the thing, Doctor, and it's exactly why I'm here. I don't know how to stop it. I've thought about it, believe me, I have spent hours, days even, just sitting in my living room, staring at the wall, trying to figure out a way to make it stop. I've thought about confronting them, maybe knocking on Mrs. Gable's door and just flat out asking her, \"Why are you doing this?\" But then I think, well, what if that just makes it worse? What if they escalate it? Because, like I said when I first came in, I *know* they're watching my comings and goings. I know it in my bones. Every time I step out into the hallway, they are keeping tabs. And those notes... I know they're slipping notes about me to the office. It's not just a feeling, it's a fact. I see the papers. And it's so frustrating because, like I told you, everybody—my sister, the manager, the maintenance guy—they all keep telling me it's just notices on the doors. They say, \"Oh, it's just about the water,\" or \"It's just the trash schedule.\" But they don't see what I see. They don't live there. They don't feel the eyes on them every time they take the trash out. So, to answer your question, I really don't know if there's a way to stop it. If the management office is in on it, or at least accepting these notes and acting like they're just routine flyers... how do you fight that? It feels like it's just going to keep happening, and I'm just going to have to keep living like a prisoner in my own apartment, jumping at every creak of the floorboards and every click of a lock. It's just... it's a lot, you know? It's just a whole lot to carry around every single day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your neighbors' behavior towards you will ever change?\n\nPatient: Change? No... no, I don't see how it could change. Why would they change? They've got this whole system figured out, you know? Like I was saying before, that's exactly why I came in here to talk to someone, because I know—I *know*—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. They've made it their full-time job. And slipping those notes about me to the office... they aren't going to just wake up one morning and decide to stop doing that. They've got a routine down. They watch, they write, they slip the note. \n\nAnd the hardest part, the part that really makes me think it's never going to stop, is that nobody else seems to care or believe me. Even though everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors... my sister, the manager, everybody. They just brush it off. \"Oh, it's just a reminder about the parking lot,\" or \"Oh, they're just painting the lines next week.\" But I know what those papers really are. I know what they're doing. And if the office is just playing along, pretending they're regular notices, why would the neighbors ever stop? They have no reason to. They're getting away with it right out in the open. \n\nI just... I sit there and I think about how long this can go on. Years? Am I going to be dealing with Mrs. Gable staring at me when I'm 70? 80? Because they don't get tired. It's like they feed off of it. Every time I open my door, I can feel the shift in the hallway, like the air gets heavy because they're all suddenly on alert. And then another note goes to the office. So no, Doctor, I don't think they'll change. I think this is just how it is now, and I'm the only one who sees it for what it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your neighbors might not actually be watching you or leaving notes about you?\n\nPatient: No, I... I can't even entertain that idea, Doctor. I just can't. Because, like I've been trying to explain, and maybe I'm just not saying it right, but I came in here specifically because I know—I absolutely know—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It's not a possibility that they aren't. It's a reality. I live it every single day. I wake up, I make my coffee, and I know that as soon as I open my door to get the paper, someone is listening. Someone is watching. \n\nAnd those notes... I mean, how can I ignore what's right in front of my face? I see them slipping notes about me to the office. I've seen them walking down to the manager's door with papers in their hands. And it's just so exhausting, because I'm the only one who gets it. Like I said, everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister, she came over last Tuesday, right? And there was a paper taped to the door across the hall, and she just glanced at it and said, \"Oh, they're checking the smoke detectors next week.\" But I tried to tell her, I said, \"No, look at the way it's folded. Look at the time they put it up.\" It's a signal. It's information about me. But she just sighed and told me I was overthinking it. \n\nBut I'm not overthinking it. I'm just paying attention. I have to pay attention, because no one else is looking out for me. If I start second-guessing myself, if I start thinking, \"Oh, maybe it really is just a notice about the water being shut off,\" then I drop my guard. And I can't drop my guard. I just can't. The evidence is there, Doctor. It's in the way Mrs. Gable pulls her curtains back just an inch when I walk down the sidewalk. It's in the way the guy with the loud truck revs his engine right when I turn off my lights at night. They are watching me, and they are reporting on me to the office. I know it. I don't just think it, I know it. So no, there's no possibility I'm wrong about this. None at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over your daily life despite what's going on with your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Control? No... no, Doctor, I don't have any control left. It's slipping right through my fingers, to be honest with you. I try, I really do try to keep things normal, but I just... I can't keep my days organized anymore. It's like my brain is just a big tangled knot. Because of all this, you know, the watching and the notes, I've fallen way behind on my bills. There's a whole stack of mail just sitting on my kitchen table, right next to the salt shaker, and I know the electric company has sent at least two past-due notices, probably the water company too, but I just can't bring myself to open them. I just sit there looking at the envelopes. \n\nAnd it's not just the bills. I've missed appointments, too. I was supposed to get my teeth looked at three weeks ago, and I had a check-up with the foot doctor, but I just didn't go. I couldn't leave the apartment. I couldn't bring myself to walk past their doors. \n\nAnd the worst part, the part that really gets to me, is that I've completely stopped showing up for church. I used to go every single Sunday, sat in the same pew near the back. I even had volunteer commitments, you know? I used to help out sorting the canned goods for the community pantry on Thursday mornings. I liked doing that, making sure the green beans and the soup were all lined up nice, handing out boxes to folks who needed them. But I had to stop all of it. I just stopped showing up. Because... well, how can I go? I can't trust ordinary interactions anymore. I go down to the pantry, and someone says 'Good morning, how are you doing?' and suddenly I'm thinking, 'Why are you asking me that? Have you been talking to Mrs. Gable? Do you know what the office is putting in my file?' I just can't trust anybody to just be a regular person anymore. It's all tainted. Every conversation feels like a trap. So I stay home. I stay home, and I miss my appointments, and the bills pile up, and the church folks probably wonder where I went, because I just can't keep my life together while all this is happening. I just don't have any control over it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts or beliefs that you think might be true, but others around you don't seem to agree with?\n\nPatient: Other thoughts? Like... like what do you mean? No, I don't think so. I mean, isn't this enough? The whole thing with the neighbors and the office, that takes up every single ounce of my energy. I don't have the time or the brainpower to be sitting around focusing on other things. I'm just a regular guy, you know? Or, well, I was a regular guy before they made my life so complicated. \n\nIf you're asking if I have any, I don't know, crazy ideas or something... no, absolutely not. I know some folks get it in their heads that they're somebody famous, or that the FBI is tapping their phones, or that the TV is sending them secret messages. I had a cousin once who thought the radio was telling him how to play the lottery, bless his heart, but I'm not like that. I don't have some wild imagination. I just see what's right in front of my face. I'm a very practical person, really. Always have been. \n\nIf Mrs. Gable would just close her curtains, and if the office would just stop taking those notes about me, I wouldn't be sitting here talking to you about any of this. I'd be back at the church pantry, sorting the canned goods, minding my own business. I'd be paying my water bill and getting my teeth checked. I don't have any other strange thoughts. There's just the truth about what's happening in my building, and that's exactly what I've been trying to get people to understand. It's just this one thing, but it's... it's everything, you know? It's my whole life right now. I can't even think about anything else because from the minute I wake up until the minute I try to sleep, I'm just trying to survive the watching. So no, Doctor, nothing else. Just them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your neighbors watching you?\n\nPatient: Evidence? What kind of evidence could there even be, Doctor? I mean, like I was trying to explain earlier, I came in here because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. I know it for a fact. I see them slipping notes about me to the office. And I know everybody keeps telling me it is just notices on the doors, you know, regular building maintenance or whatever, but they just do not understand what is really going on. How could any evidence change my mind when I am living it every single day? \n\nIf it was just regular notices, why do they only seem to put them up when I am walking down the hall? Why is it always right after I get back from the grocery store? I will come back with my bags, just trying to get my milk and my eggs in the fridge before they go bad, and there is someone hovering near the office door with a piece of paper. You just cannot show me a piece of evidence that erases what my own two eyes are seeing. People try, believe me, they try all the time. My sister, the property manager, they all try to tell me I am getting worked up over nothing, over regular flyers. But they do not live in my shoes. They do not hear the floorboards creaking above me right when I turn on the television, or see the exact way those papers are folded to hide what is written inside. \n\nSo no, I just... I do not see how anything could change my mind, because the evidence is already there proving what I am saying. I came in here hoping someone would finally understand that I am being watched, and that the notes are about me, not to be talked out of it by someone who is not even there to see it happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you imagine any situation where you might consider the possibility that your neighbors are not watching you?\n\nPatient: Imagine a situation? Doctor, I... I feel like we're just going around in circles here. I really do. How can I imagine a situation where they aren't watching me when I am living in the middle of it every single day? \n\nLike I just got done saying, I came in here today, to this very clinic, specifically because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It's not a 'possibility' I can just turn off in my head like a light switch, or some game of pretend. I walk out my door, say, to take the trash down to the chute at the end of the hall. It's a Tuesday, maybe two in the afternoon, which should be a quiet time, right? Nobody should be around. But the exact second my deadbolt clicks, there's a door cracking open down the hall. And then I see them slipping those notes about me to the office. \n\nAnd I know... believe me, I know that everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister says it, the property manager says it, and now you're asking me to imagine they aren't doing it. 'Oh, it's just a reminder about the water being shut off for maintenance,' they say. 'Oh, it's just a flyer for the community watch meeting.' A flyer? Really? You think they need to fold a community flyer three times and slide it directly into the manager's hand the exact second I walk past the stairwell? I might not have finished twelfth grade, Doctor, but I am not a stupid man. I know what a regular piece of mail looks like, and I know what people look like when they're keeping tabs on someone. \n\nSo, no. No, I absolutely cannot imagine a situation where I'd think otherwise. Because to imagine that, I'd have to pretend my own two eyes don't work. I'd have to pretend I don't see the way they stop talking when I get off the elevator. I see the watching, I see the notes going straight to the office, and I just... I can't sit here and play make-believe that it's all just innocent door notices when they are documenting my whole life. I just can't do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your neighbors watching you could be considered a fact that you hold onto strongly, even if presented with contradictory evidence?\n\nPatient: A fact? Well, yes, it is a fact. It is the absolute truth. I know you are trying to use these doctor words with me, asking if I hold onto it strongly or whatever, but... but look, like I've been telling you since I first sat down in this chair, the whole reason I came in here today is because I know—I absolutely *know*—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It is not just some belief I cooked up, it is my actual life. \n\nYou talk about contradictory evidence, but what evidence are you talking about? The things everyone else tries to tell me? Because, yes, I know everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. I hear it all the time. They say, 'Oh, it's just the exterminator schedule,' or 'Oh, it's just a reminder about the recycling bins.' But they aren't the ones standing in the hallway when Mrs. Gable cracks her door open two inches just as I'm walking to the elevator. They aren't the ones feeling those eyes on the back of their neck.\n\nI see them slipping notes about me to the office. I see the way the paper is folded up tight, and I see the looks they give the manager when they hand them over. So, yes, I hold onto it strongly, because it's what is actually happening right in front of my face. You wouldn't let someone tell you that the sky is green when you are looking right at it and seeing that it's blue, would you? That is exactly what this is like for me. Every single time I leave my apartment, every time I come back from checking my mail or just going out for a walk to clear my head, there they are. Watching. Documenting. And then passing those notes along to the office. \n\nSo no matter how many times people try to contradict me and say it's just innocent door flyers, I know the truth. I'm living the truth. I just wish someone would finally listen to me instead of trying to convince me I'm not seeing what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your neighbors' actions are part of a larger, unusual plan that most people would not believe?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well, what do you call it when a whole building is keeping track of one man? I don't know if it goes all the way to the mayor or the police or anything crazy like that, if that's what you mean by a larger plan. I'm just a regular guy, fifty-nine years old, I keep to myself, I don't bother anybody. But it takes a plan to do what they're doing, doesn't it? \n\nLike I have been saying this entire time, Doctor, the whole reason I came in here is because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. You can't just do that by accident. It takes coordination. They have to know when I'm going down to the laundry room in the basement, or when I'm walking to the corner store to get a pack of cigarettes, or when I'm taking out the trash. They have to be paying attention. \n\nAnd then, right on cue, there they are, slipping notes about me to the office. I see them do it with my own eyes. Just yesterday, I went down to check my mail—box 4B—and it took me maybe three minutes, tops. But sure enough, the guy from 4A is suddenly out there in the lobby, pretending to fiddle with his keys, and then he slides this little folded-up paper right to the manager the second my back is turned. \n\nAnd you ask if most people wouldn't believe it? Well, obviously they don't! That is the most frustrating part of this whole nightmare. Everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister, the neighbors who aren't in on it, even you keep asking me if I'm sure. They say, 'Oh, it's just the fire inspection schedule,' or 'It's just the landscaping notice for the parking lot.' But how does a landscaping notice explain them watching my door every time the deadbolt clicks? How does it explain the whispering when I get on the elevator? \n\nSo yes, maybe people don't believe it, maybe they think it's unusual or crazy, but that doesn't change the fact that I am the one living it. I'm the one trapped in the middle of it. I just don't understand why it's so hard for anyone to just listen to what I'm telling them instead of treating me like I don't know the difference between a regular building flyer and a whole group of people keeping tabs on my life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I know you are trying to use these doctor words with me, asking if I hold onto it strongly or whatever, but... but look, like I've been telling you since I first sat down in this chair, the whole reason I came in here today is because I know—I absolutely *know*—the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It is not just some belief I cooked up, it is my actual life. \n\nYou talk about contradictory evidence, but what evidence are you talking about? The things everyone else tries to tell me? Because, yes, I know everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. I hear it all the time. They say, 'Oh, it's just the exterminator schedule,' or 'Oh, it's just a reminder about the recycling bins.' But they aren't the ones standing in the hallway when Mrs. Gable cracks her door open two inches just as I'm walking to the elevator. They aren't the ones feeling those eyes on the back of their neck.\n\nI see them slipping notes about me to the office. I see the way the paper is folded up tight, and I see the looks they give the manager when they hand them over. So, yes, I hold onto it strongly, because it's what is actually happening right in front of my face. You wouldn't let someone tell you that the sky is green when you are looking right at it and seeing that it's blue, would you? That is exactly what this is like for me. Every single time I leave my apartment, every time I come back from checking my mail or just going out for a walk to clear my head, there they are. Watching. Documenting. And then passing those notes along to the office. \n\nSo no matter how many times people try to contradict me and say it's just innocent door flyers, I know the truth. I'm living the truth. I just wish someone would finally listen to me instead of trying to convince me I'm not seeing what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your neighbors' actions are part of a larger, unusual plan that most people would not believe?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well, what do you call it when a whole building is keeping track of one man? I don't know if it goes all the way to the mayor or the police or anything crazy like that, if that's what you mean by a larger plan. I'm just a regular guy, fifty-nine years old, I keep to myself, I don't bother anybody. But it takes a plan to do what they're doing, doesn't it? \n\nLike I have been saying this entire time, Doctor, the whole reason I came in here is because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. You can't just do that by accident. It takes coordination. They have to know when I'm going down to the laundry room in the basement, or when I'm walking to the corner store to get a pack of cigarettes, or when I'm taking out the trash. They have to be paying attention. \n\nAnd then, right on cue, there they are, slipping notes about me to the office. I see them do it with my own eyes. Just yesterday, I went down to check my mail—box 4B—and it took me maybe three minutes, tops. But sure enough, the guy from 4A is suddenly out there in the lobby, pretending to fiddle with his keys, and then he slides this little folded-up paper right to the manager the second my back is turned. \n\nAnd you ask if most people wouldn't believe it? Well, obviously they don't! That is the most frustrating part of this whole nightmare. Everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister, the neighbors who aren't in on it, even you keep asking me if I'm sure. They say, 'Oh, it's just the fire inspection schedule,' or 'It's just the landscaping notice for the parking lot.' But how does a landscaping notice explain them watching my door every time the deadbolt clicks? How does it explain the whispering when I get on the elevator? \n\nSo yes, maybe people don't believe it, maybe they think it's unusual or crazy, but that doesn't change the fact that I am the one living it. I'm the one trapped in the middle of it. I just don't understand why it's so hard for anyone to just listen to what I'm telling them instead of treating me like I don't know the difference between a regular building flyer and a whole group of people keeping tabs on my life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A product of my own mind? You mean... you mean you think I'm just sitting alone in my living room making all this up? Imagining things? Doctor, I... I really don't know how many different ways I can explain this to you. I feel like a broken record, but I'll say it again. 

Like I told you the second I walked through your door, the whole reason I came in here today—the only reason I am sitting in this chair talking to you right now—is because I know, for a fact, that the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It's not a dream. It's not some... some crazy story I'm spinning in my head. I see them slipping notes about me to the office. I see the actual, physical paper in their hands, Doctor. If it was just in my mind, how could I see the paper? How could I hear the hinges squeak when they open their doors just a crack to watch me walk by? 

And I know... I know everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. I get it. My sister says it, the property manager says it, the other tenants say it. They all want to brush it off and say, 'Oh, it's just the water bill,' or 'It's just the pest control schedule.' And now you're sitting there, asking if my own brain is playing tricks on me? It's insulting, to be honest with you. It really is. I might not have a high school diploma, sure, but my mind works just fine. I know what real life is. 

Real life is me trying to go about my day, just trying to live my life like any normal fifty-nine-year-old man, and these people are out there, watching every single move I make, documenting it, and handing it over to the office. It's real events. It's happening to me every single day. If it was just my mind, I could take a nap and it would stop, wouldn't it? I could just turn it off. But it doesn't stop. Because they don't stop. Every time I step out of my apartment, there they are. So no, I absolutely do not think it's possible that it's just in my mind. I'm living it. I just... I just want someone to finally believe what I am actually seeing with my own two eyes instead of telling me I'm making it all up.

It's been going on for a while now, and honestly, it's just getting to be too much. I mean, I know for a fact—absolutely certain—that the people living around me, my neighbors, they're watching me. Every time I leave my apartment, every time I come back, they're keeping track. I hear their doors click, or I see the blinds twitch. And then, and this is the part that really gets me, they're slipping these notes about me to the management office. I see the papers. They leave them on the doors, or they slide them under. Now, everybody—my sister, the building manager, even the maintenance guy—they all keep telling me, \"Oh, it's just routine notices, it's just reminders about the trash or the water being shut off.\" But I know better. I can read between the lines. I know it's about me, tracking my comings and goings, reporting on me. It's just exhausting, you know? Always having to be on guard, always wondering who's writing what about you. I just couldn't take it anymore, so I figured I had to come talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear, related to these notes or your neighbors watching you?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I haven't heard any voices or anything like that. It's not like someone is whispering in my ear or shouting my name when no one is there. I mean, I hear the actual sounds they make, like I said, the doors clicking shut right as I walk past, or the floorboards creaking upstairs when they're pacing, waiting for me to leave. But it's not like... imaginary voices or sounds that aren't really there. I know some people get that, where they hear things, but for me, it's all very real, very physical things happening right in front of me. The notes are right there on the paper, the blinds are actually moving. I don't need to hear voices to know what they're doing because they're so obvious about it, you know? It's the actions, the constant surveillance, the little noises they make when they think I'm not paying attention. But no, no voices talking to me or anything like that. Just the reality of what they're up to, which is honestly loud enough on its own without needing any extra sounds in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there are any other plots or plans against you beyond your neighbors watching you and leaving notes?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think so. It's really just the people in the building. I mean, I've racked my brain trying to figure out if there's some bigger picture here, you know? Like, is it the city? Is it the government? But honestly, I don't see any evidence of that. It's just the neighbors. Like Mrs. Gable in 3B, she's always at her window. And that guy on the first floor, the one with the loud truck, he's definitely part of it. But I haven't noticed anyone following me when I go to the grocery store or anything like that. It's really localized to the building. Which almost makes it worse, if you think about it, because it's where I live. It's my home. I should feel safe there, but instead, I'm constantly analyzing every little thing they do. But outside of the building, no, I haven't seen any signs of a larger plot or anything. It's just those people, right there, right where I have to sleep and eat and try to live my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop your neighbors from watching you and leaving these notes, or do you think this is something that will continue no matter what?\n\nPatient: Well, see, that's the thing, Doctor, and it's exactly why I'm here. I don't know how to stop it. I've thought about it, believe me, I have spent hours, days even, just sitting in my living room, staring at the wall, trying to figure out a way to make it stop. I've thought about confronting them, maybe knocking on Mrs. Gable's door and just flat out asking her, \"Why are you doing this?\" But then I think, well, what if that just makes it worse? What if they escalate it? Because, like I said when I first came in, I *know* they're watching my comings and goings. I know it in my bones. They say, \"Oh, it's just about the water,\" or \"It's just the trash schedule.\" But they don't see what I see. They don't live there. They don't feel the eyes on them every time they take the trash out. So, to answer your question, I really don't know if there's a way to stop it. If the management office is in on it, or at least accepting these notes and acting like they're just routine flyers... how do you fight that? It feels like it's just going to keep happening, and I'm just going to have to keep living like a prisoner in my own apartment, jumping at every creak of the floorboards and every click of a lock. It's just... it's a lot, you know? It's just a whole lot to carry around every single day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your neighbors' behavior towards you will ever change?\n\nPatient: Change? No... no, I don't see how it could change. Why would they change? They've got this whole system figured out, you know? They've made it their full-time job. And slipping those notes about me to the office... they aren't going to just wake up one morning and decide to stop doing that. They've got a routine down. They watch, they write, they slip the note. \n\nAnd the hardest part, the part that really makes me think it's never going to stop, is that nobody else seems to care or believe me. Even though everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors... my sister, the manager, everybody. They just brush it off. \"Oh, it's just a reminder about the parking lot,\" or \"Oh, they're just painting the lines next week.\" But I know what those papers really are. I know what they're doing. And if the office is just playing along, pretending they're regular notices, why would the neighbors ever stop? They have no reason to. They're getting away with it right out in the open. \n\nI just... I sit there and I think about how long this can go on. Years? Am I going to be dealing with Mrs. Gable staring at me when I'm 70? 80? Because they don't get tired. It's like they feed off of it. Every time I open my door, I can feel the shift in the hallway, like the air gets heavy because they're all suddenly on alert. And then another note goes to the office. So no, Doctor, I don't think they'll change. I think this is just how it is now, and I'm the only one who sees it for what it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your neighbors might not actually be watching you or leaving notes about you?\n\nPatient: No, I... Like I said, everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors. My sister, she came over last Tuesday, right? And there was a paper taped to the door across the hall, and she just glanced at it and said, \"Oh, they're checking the smoke detectors next week.\" But I tried to tell her, I said, \"No, look at the way it's folded. I have to pay attention, because no one else is looking out for me. If I start second-guessing myself, if I start thinking, \"Oh, maybe it really is just a notice about the water being shut off,\" then I drop my guard. And I can't drop my guard. I just can't. The evidence is there, Doctor. It's in the way Mrs. Gable pulls her curtains back just an inch when I walk down the sidewalk. It's in the way the guy with the loud truck revs his engine right when I turn off my lights at night. They are watching me, and they are reporting on me to the office. I know it. I don't just think it, I know it. So no, there's no possibility I'm wrong about this. It's like my brain is just a big tangled knot. Because of all this, you know, the watching and the notes, I've fallen way behind on my bills. I just sit there looking at the envelopes. \n\nAnd it's not just the bills. I've missed appointments, too. I was supposed to get my teeth looked at three weeks ago, and I had a check-up with the foot doctor, but I just didn't go. I couldn't leave the apartment. I couldn't bring myself to walk past their doors. \n\nAnd the worst part, the part that really gets to me, is that I've completely stopped showing up for church. I used to go every single Sunday, sat in the same pew near the back. I even had volunteer commitments, you know? I liked doing that, making sure the green beans and the soup were all lined up nice, handing out boxes to folks who needed them. But I had to stop all of it. I just stopped showing up. I stay home, and I miss my appointments, and the bills pile up, and the church folks probably wonder where I went, because I just can't keep my life together while all this is happening. I just don't have any control over it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts or beliefs that you think might be true, but others around you don't seem to agree with?\n\nPatient: Other thoughts? They do not hear the floorboards creaking above me right when I turn on the television, or see the exact way those papers are folded to hide what is written inside. \n\nSo no, I just... I do not see how anything could change my mind, because the evidence is already there proving what I am saying. I came in here hoping someone would finally understand that I am being watched, and that the notes are about me, not to be talked out of it by someone who is not even there to see it happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you imagine any situation where you might consider the possibility that your neighbors are not watching you?\n\nPatient: Imagine a situation? Doctor, I... I feel like we're just going around in circles here. I really do. 'Oh, it's just a flyer for the community watch meeting.' A flyer? Really? I know what a regular piece of mail looks like, and I know what people look like when they're keeping tabs on someone. \n\nSo, no.
